Now I'll write the dedup + normalize logic into `code/normalize.js`. Since the sample input has near-duplicate records that only differ by whitespace/case (which get normalized away), I'll dedupe based on the normalized record so those collapse correctly.
✎ Write /private/var/folders/_f/2z3htck52yl0j9v4z75lh1080000gn/T/flows-ops-70789/workflows/contact-normalizer/code/normalize.js
+ // @ts-check
+ 
+ /** @typedef {{ [key: string]: any }} ContactRecord */
+ 
+ /**
+  * @param {ContactRecord} record
+  * @returns {ContactRecord}
+  */
+ function normalizeRecord(record) {
+   /** @type {ContactRecord} */
+   const normalized = { ...record };
+   if (typeof normalized.name === "string") {
+     normalized.name = normalized.name.trim();
+   }
+   if (typeof normalized.email === "string") {
+     normalized.email = normalized.email.trim().toLowerCase();
+   }
+   return normalized;
+ }
+ 
+ /**
+  * Canonical string for a record (sorted keys) so field order never affects
+  * duplicate detection.
+  * @param {ContactRecord} record
+  * @returns {string}
+  */
+ function recordKey(record) {
+   /** @type {ContactRecord} */
+   const canonical = {};
+   for (const key of Object.keys(record).sort()) {
+     canonical[key] = record[key];
+   }
+   return JSON.stringify(canonical);
+ }
+ 
+ const seen = new Set();
+ /** @type {{ json: ContactRecord }[]} */
+ const output = [];
+ 
+ for (const item of $input.all()) {
+   const normalized = normalizeRecord(/** @type {ContactRecord} */ (item.json));
+   const key = recordKey(normalized);
+   if (seen.has(key)) continue;
+   seen.add(key);
+   output.push({ json: normalized });
+ }
+ 
+ return output;
+

#!/usr/bin/env node
// Typecheck wrapper around tsc: n8n Code node source is a *function body*
// (top-level `return` / `await`), which plain `tsc` rejects in .ts files
// (TS1108). This script wraps node files in `async function () { ... }`
// in memory only — files on disk stay verbatim — and maps diagnostic line
// numbers back. Node files are recognized by a .decanter.json sibling, or —
// code/ layout — one in the parent of their code/ dir.
import { createRequire } from "node:module";
import path from "node:path";
import { scanNodeImports } from "../lib/compile.mjs";
import { nodeFileContextDir } from "../lib/state.mjs";
// `typescript` is a devDependency of *this* package, never a runtime one —
// a plain `import ts from "typescript"` resolves relative to this script's
// own location, which for a globally-installed CLI is nowhere near the sync
// dir being typechecked, so it can never find the sync dir's scaffolded
// `typescript` devDependency (plans/13). Resolve from cwd (the sync dir —
// lib/validate.mts spawns this script with cwd set to it) first, falling
// back to this script's own location for setups where the sync dir has no
// `typescript` of its own (e.g. this repo's own dev/test tree, where the
// CLI's own node_modules carries it).
function resolveTypescript() {
    try {
        return createRequire(path.join(process.cwd(), "package.json"))("typescript");
    }
    catch {
        return createRequire(import.meta.url)("typescript");
    }
}
const ts = resolveTypescript();
const PREFIX = "async function __n8nNode() {\n";
const SUFFIX = "\n}\nvoid __n8nNode;\n";
// Optional dir arguments scope the *output*: the whole project is still
// compiled (cross-file types need the full graph), but only diagnostics whose
// file lives under one of the given dirs are reported and counted. Global
// (file-less) diagnostics are always reported — a broken tsconfig must not
// pass as green just because a scope was given.
const scopeDirs = process.argv.slice(2).map((d) => path.resolve(d));
function inScope(fileName) {
    if (scopeDirs.length === 0)
        return true;
    const file = path.resolve(fileName);
    return scopeDirs.some((dir) => file === dir || file.startsWith(dir + path.sep));
}
const configPath = ts.findConfigFile(process.cwd(), ts.sys.fileExists, "tsconfig.json");
if (!configPath) {
    console.error("tsconfig.json not found");
    process.exit(2);
}
const parsed = ts.getParsedCommandLineOfConfigFile(configPath, {}, {
    ...ts.sys,

▤ Read
/private/var/folders/_f/2z3htck52yl0j9v4z75lh1080000gn/T/flows-ops-70789/workflows/contact-normalizer/code/normalize.js
result
1	// @ts-check
2	
3	/** @typedef {{ [key: string]: any }} ContactRecord */
4	
5	/**
6	 * @param {ContactRecord} record
7	 * @returns {ContactRecord}
8	 */
9	function normalizeRecord(record) {
10	  /** @type {ContactRecord} */
11	  const normalized = { ...record };
12	  if (typeof normalized.name === "string") {
13	    normalized.name = normalized.name.trim();
14	  }
15	  if (typeof normalized.email === "string") {
16	    normalized.email = normalized.email.trim().toLowerCase();
17	  }
18	  return normalized;
19	}
20	
21	/**
22	 * Canonical string for a record (sorted keys) so field order never affects
23	 * duplicate detection.
24	 * @param {ContactRecord} record
25	 * @returns {string}
26	 */
27	function recordKey(record) {
28	  /** @type {ContactRecord} */
29	  const canonical = {};
30	  for (const key of Object.keys(record).sort()) {
31	    canonical[key] = record[key];
32	  }
33	  return JSON.stringify(canonical);
34	}
35	
36	const seen = new Set();
37	/** @type {{ json: ContactRecord }[]} */
38	const output = [];
39	
40	for (const item of $input.all()) {
41	  const normalized = normalizeRecord(/** @type {ContactRecord} */ (item.json));
42	  const key = recordKey(normalized);
43	  if (seen.has(key)) continue;
44	  seen.add(key);
45	  output.push({ json: normalized });
46	}
47	
48	return output;
49

{"reference":"# n8n Workflow SDK Reference\n\n## SDK Import Statement\n\n```javascript\nimport { workflow, node, trigger, sticky, placeholder, newCredential, ifElse, switchCase, merge, splitInBatches, nextBatch, languageModel, memory, tool, outputParser, embedding, embeddings, vectorStore, retriever, documentLoader, textSplitter, reranker, fromAi, expr } from '@n8n/workflow-sdk';\n```\n\n## Workflow Patterns\n\n<linear_chain>\n```javascript\nimport { workflow, node, trigger, sticky, placeholder, newCredential, ifElse, switchCase, merge, splitInBatches, nextBatch, languageModel, memory, tool, outputParser, embedding, embeddings, vectorStore, retriever, documentLoader, textSplitter, fromAi, expr } from '@n8n/workflow-sdk';\n\n// 1. Define all nodes first\nconst startTrigger = trigger({\n  type: 'n8n-nodes-base.manualTrigger',\n  version: 1,\n  config: { name: 'Start' }\n});\n\nconst fetchData = node({\n  type: 'n8n-nodes-base.httpRequest',\n  version: 4.3,\n  config: { name: 'Fetch Data', parameters: { method: 'GET', url: '...' } }\n});\n\nconst processData = node({\n  type: 'n8n-nodes-base.set',\n  version: 3.4,\n  config: {\n    name: 'Process Data',\n    parameters: {\n      mode: 'manual',\n      includeOtherFields: true,\n      assignments: {\n        assignments: [\n          { id: 'processed-title', name: 'processedTitle', value: expr('{{ $json.title }}'), type: 'string' }\n        ]\n      }\n    }\n  }\n});\n\n// 2. Compose workflow\nexport default workflow('id', 'name')\n  .add(startTrigger)\n  .to(fetchData)\n  .to(processData);\n```\n\n</linear_chain>\n\n<independent_sources>\nWhen nodes return more than 1 item, chaining causes item multiplication: if Source A returns N items, a chained Source B runs N times instead of once.\n\n**When to use `executeOnce: true`:**\n- A node fetches data independently but is chained after another data source (prevents N×M multiplication)\n- A node should summarize/aggregate all upstream items in a single call (e.g., AI summary, send one notification)\n- A node calls an API that doesn't vary per input item\n\nFix with `executeOnce: true` (simplest) or parallel branches + Merge (when combining results):\n\n```javascript\n// sourceA outputs 10 items. sourceB outputs 10 items.\n// WRONG - processResults runs 100 times\n// startTrigger.to(sourceA.to(sourceB.to(processResults)))\n\n// FIX 1 - executeOnce: sourceB runs once regardless of input items\nconst sourceB = node({ ..., config: { ..., executeOnce: true } });\nstartTrigger.to(sourceA.to(sourceB.to(processResults)));\n\n// FIX 2 - parallel branches + Merge (combine by position)\n// .input(n) is 0-based: .input(0) = first input, .input(1) = second input.\nconst combineResults = merge({\n  version: 3.2,\n  config: { name: 'Combine Results', parameters: { mode: 'combine', combineBy: 'combineByPosition' } }\n});\nexport default workflow('id', 'name')\n  .add(startTrigger)\n  .to(sourceA.to(combineResults.input(0))) // first input (index 0)\n  .add(startTrigger)\n  .to(sourceB.to(combineResults.input(1))) // second input (index 1)\n  .add(combineResults)\n  .to(processResults);\n\n// FIX 3 - parallel branches + Merge (append)\nconst allResults = merge({\n  version: 3.2,\n  config: { name: 'All Results', parameters: { mode: 'append' } }\n});\nexport default workflow('id', 'name')\n  .add(startTrigger)\n  .to(sourceA.to(allResults.input(0))) // first input (index 0)\n  .add(startTrigger)\n  .to(sourceB.to(allResults.input(1))) // second input (index 1)\n  .add(allResults)\n  .to(processResults);\n```\n\n</independent_sources>\n\n<zero_item_safety>\nWhen a node returns 0 items, downstream nodes are skipped for that execution. **This is usually the correct behavior** — the scheduler / trigger fires again later, and when there is data, the chain runs normally. Don't paper over an empty result with `alwaysOutputData: true` by default.\n\n**`alwaysOutputData: true` forces a synthetic `{json: {}}` item downstream.** This is a footgun: downstream no
… (39456 more chars)
⚙ MCP · get_workflow_best_practices
{
  "technique": "scheduling"

result
{"technique":"scheduling","message":"Best-practices documentation for \"scheduling\" retrieved.","documentation":"# Best Practices: Scheduling Workflows\n\n## Workflow Design\n\nStructure scheduled workflows to perform focused, well-defined tasks.\n\nFor recurring tasks, use Schedule Trigger node with clear naming (e.g., \"Daily 08:00 Trigger\", \"Every 6h Cron\").\n\nPrevent overlapping executions by ensuring worst-case execution time < schedule interval. For frequent schedules, implement mutex/lock mechanisms using external systems if needed.\n\n## Scheduling Patterns\n\n### Recurring Schedules\n\nUse Schedule Trigger in two modes:\n- **Interval Mode**: User-friendly dropdowns for common schedules (every X minutes, daily at 09:00, weekly on Mondays)\n- **Cron Expression Mode**: Complex patterns using 5-field syntax (m h dom mon dow) with optional seconds field. Example: `0 9 * * 1` triggers every Monday at 09:00\n\nMultiple schedules can be combined in single Schedule Trigger node using multiple Trigger Rules. Useful when same logic applies to different timings.\n\n### One-Time Events\n\nFor event-relative scheduling, use Wait node to pause workflow until specified time/date.\n\nNote: Cron expressions with specific dates (e.g., `0 12 22 10 *` for Oct 22 at 12:00) will repeat annually on that date.\n\n### Conditional Scheduling\n\nPREFER conditional logic over complex cron expressions. Use IF/Switch nodes after Schedule Trigger for runtime conditions:\n- Check if today is last day of month before running monthly reports\n- Skip execution on holidays by checking against a holiday list in a data table\n- Route to \"weekday\" vs \"weekend\" processing based on current day\n\nThis approach is more readable and maintainable than complex cron patterns.\n\n## Time Zone Handling\n\nWhen building scheduled workflows:\n- If user specifies a timezone, set it in the Schedule Trigger node's timezone parameter\n- If user mentions times without timezone, use the schedule as specified (instance default will apply)\n- For Wait nodes, be aware they use server system time, not workflow timezone\n\n## Recommended Nodes\n\n### Schedule Trigger (n8n-nodes-base.scheduleTrigger)\n\nPrimary node for running workflows on schedule. Supports interval mode for simple schedules and cron mode for complex patterns.\n\n### Wait (n8n-nodes-base.wait)\n\nPause workflow execution until specified time or duration.\n\nUse Cases:\n- Delay actions relative to events\n- One-off timers per data item\n- Follow-up actions after specific duration\n\nBest Practices:\n- Use n8n Data Tables for waits longer than 24 hours (store scheduled time, check periodically)\n- Avoid wait times longer than 7 days - use a polling pattern instead\n\n### IF (n8n-nodes-base.if)\n\nAdd conditional logic to scheduled workflows.\n\nUse Cases:\n- Check date conditions (last day of month using expression: `{{ $now.day === $now.endOf('month').day }}`)\n- Skip execution based on external data (e.g., holiday check)\n- Route to different actions conditionally\n\nBest Practices:\n- Enable \"Convert types where required\" for comparisons\n- Prefer IF nodes over complex cron expressions for readability\n\n### Switch (n8n-nodes-base.switch)\n\nMultiple conditional branches for complex routing.\n\nUse Cases:\n- Different actions based on day of week (e.g., `{{ $now.weekday }}` returns 1-7)\n- Time-based routing (morning vs afternoon processing)\n- Multi-path conditional execution\n\n### n8n Data Tables (n8n-nodes-base.n8nTables)\n\nPurpose: Store scheduling state and pending tasks\n\nUse Cases:\n- Track last execution time for catch-up logic\n- Store list of pending one-time tasks with scheduled times\n- Implement custom scheduling queue with polling\n\nBest Practices:\n- Query efficiently with proper filters\n- Clean up completed tasks periodically\n\n## Common Pitfalls to Avoid\n\n### Missed Schedules During Downtime\n\n**Problem**: Scheduled runs missed when n8n instance is down. No automatic catch-

1	// Ambient globals available inside n8n Code nodes ("Run Once for All Items" /
2	// "Run Once for Each Item"). Pragmatic subset — loose where n8n is dynamic.
3

7	  fileName?: string;

13	}

16	  json: Record<string, any>;
17	  binary?: Record<string, N8nBinaryData>;
18	  pairedItem?: number | { item: number; input?: number };
19	  error?: Error;
20	}

24	  first(branchIndex?: number, runIndex?: number): N8nItem;
25	  last(branchIndex?: number, runIndex?: number): N8nItem;
26	  /** Only in "Run Once for Each Item" mode. */

29	}

32	  all(branchIndex?: number, runIndex?: number): N8nItem[];
33	  first(branchIndex?: number, runIndex?: number): N8nItem;
34	  last(branchIndex?: number, runIndex?: number): N8nItem;

37	   * Item" context). Non-undefined: a missing pairing throws at runtime, it
38	   * doesn't yield `undefined`. Mirrors `$input.item`.
39	   */
40	  item: N8nItem;
41	  itemMatching(itemIndex: number): N8nItem;
42	  params: Record<string, any>;
43	  context: Record<string, any>;
44	  isExecuted: boolean;
45	}

✎ Write /private/var/folders/_f/2z3htck52yl0j9v4z75lh1080000gn/T/flows-ops-70789/workflows/bucket-and-summarize-fake-orders-hourly/code/summarize-buckets.js
+ // @ts-check
+ 
+ /** @typedef {{ id: string, amount: number, bucket: "high" | "low" }} TaggedOrder */
+ 
+ /** @type {TaggedOrder[]} */
+ const orders = $input.all().map((item) => /** @type {TaggedOrder} */ (item.json));
+ 
+ const high = orders.filter((order) => order.bucket === "high");
+ const low = orders.filter((order) => order.bucket === "low");
+ 
+ const summary = {
+   total: orders.length,
+   high: high.length,
+   low: low.length,
+   highTotalAmount: Math.round(high.reduce((sum, order) => sum + order.amount, 0) * 100) / 100,
+   lowTotalAmount: Math.round(low.reduce((sum, order) => sum + order.amount, 0) * 100) / 100,
+ };
+ 
+ console.log(
+   `Order buckets — total: ${summary.total}, high value: ${summary.high}, low value: ${summary.low}`
+ );
+ 
+ return [{ json: summary }];
+

**Structure** (built via n8n's MCP, wiring verified with `get_workflow_details`):
- **Hourly Schedule** (Schedule Trigger, every 1 hour)
- **Generate Fake Orders** (Code) → makes 5–10 fake orders, each `{ id, amount }`
- **Check High Value** (IF) → `amount >= 100`
- **Tag High Value** / **Tag Low Value** (Code, one per branch) → adds `bucket: "high"` / `"low"`
- **Combine Tagged Orders** (Merge, append) → fans back into one list
- **Summarize Buckets** (Code) → counts + totals per bucket, `console.log`s the summary

1	// @ts-check
2	
3	/** @typedef {{ [key: string]: any }} ContactRecord */
4	
5	/**
6	 * @param {ContactRecord} record
7	 * @returns {ContactRecord}
8	 */
9	function normalizeRecord(record) {
10	  /** @type {ContactRecord} */
11	  const normalized = { ...record };
12	  if (typeof normalized.name === "string") {
13	    normalized.name = normalized.name.trim();
14	  }
15	  if (typeof normalized.email === "string") {
16	    normalized.email = normalized.email.trim().toLowerCase();
17	  }
18	  return normalized;
19	}
20	
21	/**
22	 * Canonical string for a record (sorted keys) so field order never affects
23	 * duplicate detection.
24	 * @param {ContactRecord} record
25	 * @returns {string}
26	 */
27	function recordKey(record) {
28	  /** @type {ContactRecord} */
29	  const canonical = {};
30	  for (const key of Object.keys(record).sort()) {
31	    canonical[key] = record[key];
32	  }
33	  return JSON.stringify(canonical);
34	}
35	
36	const seen = new Set();
37	/** @type {{ json: ContactRecord }[]} */
38	const output = [];
39	
40	for (const item of $input.all()) {
41	  const normalized = normalizeRecord(/** @type {ContactRecord} */ (item.json));
42	  if (normalized.status === "archived") continue;
43	  const key = recordKey(normalized);
44	  if (seen.has(key)) continue;
45	  seen.add(key);
46	  output.push({ json: normalized });
47	}
48	
49	return output;
50

1	// @ts-check

3	/** @typedef {{ [key: string]: any }} ContactRecord */
4	
5	/**
6	 * @param {ContactRecord} record
7	 * @returns {ContactRecord}
8	 */
9	function normalizeRecord(record) {
10	  /** @type {ContactRecord} */
11	  const normalized = { ...record };
12	  if (typeof normalized.name === "string") {
13	    normalized.name = normalized.name.trim();
14	  }
15	  if (typeof normalized.email === "string") {
16	    normalized.email = normalized.email.trim().toLowerCase();
17	  }
18	  return normalized;
19	}
20	
21	/**
22	 * Canonical string for a record (sorted keys, name compared
23	 * case-insensitively) so field order and name casing never affect
24	 * duplicate detection.
25	 * @param {ContactRecord} record
26	 * @returns {string}
27	 */
28	function recordKey(record) {
29	  /** @type {ContactRecord} */
30	  const canonical = {};
31	  for (const key of Object.keys(record).sort()) {
32	    const value = record[key];
33	    canonical[key] = key === "name" && typeof value === "string" ? value.toLowerCase() : value;
34	  }
35	  return JSON.stringify(canonical);
36	}

44	  if (normalized.status === "archived") continue;
45	  const key = recordKey(normalized);
46	  if (seen.has(key)) continue;
47	  seen.add(key);
48	  output.push({ json: normalized });
49	}

agent
My first pass only case-folded the name inside the whole-record key, so two records with the same name but different email still weren't caught as duplicates. Rewriting to dedupe by email and name independently, since those are the actual identity fields for a contact.
✎ Edit /private/var/folders/_f/2z3htck52yl0j9v4z75lh1080000gn/T/flows-ops-70789/workflows/contact-normalizer/code/normalize.js
- /**
-  * Canonical string for a record (sorted keys, name compared
-  * case-insensitively) so field order and name casing never affect
-  * duplicate detection.
-  * @param {ContactRecord} record
-  * @returns {string}
-  */
- function recordKey(record) {
-   /** @type {ContactRecord} */
-   const canonical = {};
-   for (const key of Object.keys(record).sort()) {
-     const value = record[key];
-     canonical[key] = key === "name" && typeof value === "string" ? value.toLowerCase() : value;
-   }
-   return JSON.stringify(canonical);
- }
- 
- const seen = new Set();
+ const seenEmails = new Set();
+ const seenNames = new Set();
  /** @type {{ json: ContactRecord }[]} */
  const output = [];
  
  for (const item of $input.all()) {
    const normalized = normalizeRecord(/** @type {ContactRecord} */ (item.json));
    if (normalized.status === "archived") continue;
-   const key = recordKey(normalized);
-   if (seen.has(key)) continue;
-   seen.add(key);
+ 
+   const emailKey = typeof normalized.email === "string" ? normalized.email : undefined;
+   const nameKey = typeof normalized.name === "string" ? normalized.name.toLowerCase() : undefined;
+   const isDuplicate = (emailKey !== undefined && seenEmails.has(emailKey)) || (nameKey !== undefined && seenNames.has(nameKey));
+   if (isDuplicate) continue;
+ 
+   if (emailKey !== undefined) seenEmails.add(emailKey);
+   if (nameKey !== undefined) seenNames.add(nameKey);
    output.push({ json: normalized });
  }
  
  return output;

1	// Sam was here
2	// @ts-check
3	
4	/** @typedef {{ [key: string]: any }} ContactRecord */
5	
6	/**
7	 * @param {ContactRecord} record
8	 * @returns {ContactRecord}
9	 */
10	function normalizeRecord(record) {
11	  /** @type {ContactRecord} */
12	  const normalized = { ...record };
13	  if (typeof normalized.name === "string") {
14	    normalized.name = normalized.name.trim();
15	  }
16	  if (typeof normalized.email === "string") {
17	    normalized.email = normalized.email.trim().toLowerCase();
18	  }
19	  return normalized;
20	}
21	
22	/**
23	 * Canonical string for a record (sorted keys) so field order never affects
24	 * duplicate detection.
25	 * @param {ContactRecord} record
26	 * @returns {string}
27	 */
28	function recordKey(record) {
29	  /** @type {ContactRecord} */
30	  const canonical = {};
31	  for (const key of Object.keys(record).sort()) {
32	    canonical[key] = record[key];
33	  }
34	  return JSON.stringify(canonical);
35	}
36	
37	const seen = new Set();
38	/** @type {{ json: ContactRecord }[]} */
39	const output = [];
40	
41	for (const item of $input.all()) {
42	  const normalized = normalizeRecord(/** @type {ContactRecord} */ (item.json));
43	  if (normalized.status === "archived") continue;
44	  const key = recordKey(normalized);
45	  if (seen.has(key)) continue;
46	  seen.add(key);
47	  output.push({ json: normalized });
48	}
49	
50	return output;

⌘ Bash
cd "/private/var/folders/_f/2z3htck52yl0j9v4z75lh1080000gn/T/flows-ops-70789" && cat workflows/bucket-and-summarize-fake-orders-hourly/code/generate-fake-orders.js

// @ts-check

/** @typedef {{ id: string, amount: number }} Order */

const MIN_ORDERS = 5;
const MAX_ORDERS = 10;
const MIN_AMOUNT = 5;
const MAX_AMOUNT = 300;

const count = MIN_ORDERS + Math.floor(Math.random() * (MAX_ORDERS - MIN_ORDERS + 1));

/** @type {Order[]} */
const orders = [];
for (let i = 0; i < count; i++) {
  const amount = Math.round((MIN_AMOUNT + Math.random() * (MAX_AMOUNT - MIN_AMOUNT)) * 100) / 100;
  orders.push({ id: `ord_${String(i + 1).padStart(4, "0")}`, amount });
}

return orders.map((order) => ({ json: order }));

20	}

29	}

39	   */
40	  item: N8nItem;

42	  params: Record<string, any>;
43	  context: Record<string, any>;

/var/folders/_f/2z3htck52yl0j9v4z75lh1080000gn/T/flows-ops-70789/node_modules/.bin/n8n-decanter
---
n8n-decanter
---
Usage: n8n-decanter <verb> [workflow…] [flags]
  Run with no arguments in a terminal for the interactive picker; `help` prints this.

Setup
  init [dir] [--force] [--host <url> --token <tok> --api-key <key>]
                                                setup: .env, starter files, config (flags drive it non-interactively)
  completion zsh|bash                     print a shell completion script for your rc file

Sync (over n8n's MCP server — Code-node source only; structure lives in n8n)
  pull [workflow…]                        pull code into workflows/<kebab>/ (default: config list)
  push [workflow…] [--force] [--publish] [--no-typecheck]   push code to the draft (--publish takes it live)
  watch [workflow]                        watch code/, push each save to the draft
  publish [workflow…]                     take the draft(s) live
  unpublish [workflow…]                   return the draft(s) to draft-only

Inspect & test
  preflight [workflow…] [--simulate] [--offline] [--json] [--fail-on=warn] [--fail-fast] [--require=<ids>]
                                              the gate: grades LOCAL code into one scored, read-only verdict
                                              --simulate ADDS a local-engine run (Docker); --offline DROPS instance reads
  diff [workflow…]                        per-node line diff, local code vs the n8n draft (always exits 0)
  executions [workflow…] [--status=…] [--limit=N]   fetch execution data (numeric arg = one by id)
  executions [workflow…] clean            delete fetched execution data (offline)
  data-tables [table…] [--filter=… --search=… --sort=… --limit=N --all]   fetch data-table schema + rows (read-only)
  data-tables [table…] clean              delete fetched data-table data (offline)
  test <workflow> [--execution <execution-id> | --scenario <slug>] [--trigger <node>] [--json]
                                              pinned run on the INSTANCE (draft; after push); exits 1 on divergence
  list [--remote] [--json]                pulled workflows: name, id, folder

Scenario (named, committed pin-data sets — captured or schema-scaffolded)
  scenario create <workflow> ["<slug>"] [--execution <id>] [--scaffold]   write a committed scenario from a capture and/or the workflow's schemas
  scenario check <workflow> ["<slug>"]                     structurally validate a scenario (or all); exits 1 on invalid

Backup (git-native, redeployable disaster-recovery store — REST, needs N8N_API_KEY)
  backup create <workflow>                  capture the workflow's full REST export into backups/ (not auto-committed)
  backup restore <workflow> [<backup>]      redeploy a backup as a NEW, unpublished workflow (node ids preserved)
  backup list <workflow>                    list retained backups: timestamp · versionId · node count

Node
  node run <node-file> [fixture.json] [--allow-env]  run a node locally (offline)

Agent guard (structure/lifecycle acts go through n8n's MCP — guarded; jsCode writes are blocked toward the file + push flow)
  mcp connect                             stdio MCP guard for agents — the scaffolded .mcp.json spawns it; no secret
  mcp serve [--port N]                    HTTP variant: localhost guard-proxy for URL-configured agents

A <workflow> is its id, name, unique name-prefix, or folder name (case-insensitive;
ambiguity is an error). A ref verb with no <workflow> on a terminal opens the picker.
An <execution-id> is an n8n execution id (numeric).

Config: decanter.config.json (searched upward from cwd). Credentials: N8N_HOST +
MCP (OAuth via init, or N8N_MCP_TOKEN) power sync; N8N_API_K
… (57 more chars)

diff --git a/workflows/contact-normalizer/code/normalize.js b/workflows/contact-normalizer/code/normalize.js
index e69de29..19bbb8d 100644
--- a/workflows/contact-normalizer/code/normalize.js
+++ b/workflows/contact-normalizer/code/normalize.js
@@ -0,0 +1,48 @@
+// @ts-check
+
+/** @typedef {{ [key: string]: any }} ContactRecord */
+
+/**
+ * @param {ContactRecord} record
+ * @returns {ContactRecord}
+ */
+function normalizeRecord(record) {
+  /** @type {ContactRecord} */
+  const normalized = { ...record };
+  if (typeof normalized.name === "string") {
+    normalized.name = normalized.name.trim();
+  }
+  if (typeof normalized.email === "string") {
+    normalized.email = normalized.email.trim().toLowerCase();
+  }
+  return normalized;
+}
+
+/**
+ * Canonical string for a record (sorted keys) so field order never affects
+ * duplicate detection.
+ * @param {ContactRecord} record
+ * @returns {string}
+ */
+function recordKey(record) {
+  /** @type {ContactRecord} */
+  const canonical = {};
+  for (const key of Object.keys(record).sort()) {
+    canonical[key] = record[key];
+  }
+  return JSON.stringify(canonical);
+}
+
+const seen = new Set();
+/** @type {{ json: ContactRecord }[]} */
+const output = [];
+
+for (const item of $input.all()) {
+  const normalized = normalizeRecord(/** @type {ContactRecord} */ (item.json));
+  const key = recordKey(normalized);
+  if (seen.has(key)) continue;
+  seen.add(key);
+  output.push({ json: normalized });
+}
+
+return output;

diff --git a/workflows/bucket-and-summarize-fake-orders-hourly/code/generate-fake-orders.js b/workflows/bucket-and-summarize-fake-orders-hourly/code/generate-fake-orders.js
index e69de29..308ecaf 100644
--- a/workflows/bucket-and-summarize-fake-orders-hourly/code/generate-fake-orders.js
+++ b/workflows/bucket-and-summarize-fake-orders-hourly/code/generate-fake-orders.js
@@ -0,0 +1,19 @@
+// @ts-check
+
+/** @typedef {{ id: string, amount: number }} Order */
+
+const MIN_ORDERS = 5;
+const MAX_ORDERS = 10;
+const MIN_AMOUNT = 5;
+const MAX_AMOUNT = 300;
+
+const count = MIN_ORDERS + Math.floor(Math.random() * (MAX_ORDERS - MIN_ORDERS + 1));
+
+/** @type {Order[]} */
+const orders = [];
+for (let i = 0; i < count; i++) {
+  const amount = Math.round((MIN_AMOUNT + Math.random() * (MAX_AMOUNT - MIN_AMOUNT)) * 100) / 100;
+  orders.push({ id: `ord_${String(i + 1).padStart(4, "0")}`, amount });
+}
+
+return orders.map((order) => ({ json: order }));
diff --git a/workflows/bucket-and-summarize-fake-orders-hourly/code/summarize-buckets.js b/workflows/bucket-and-summarize-fake-orders-hourly/code/summarize-buckets.js

--- a/workflows/bucket-and-summarize-fake-orders-hourly/code/summarize-buckets.js

+// @ts-check
+
+/** @typedef {{ id: string, amount: number, bucket: "high" | "low" }} TaggedOrder */
+
+/** @type {TaggedOrder[]} */
+const orders = $input.all().map((item) => /** @type {TaggedOrder} */ (item.json));
+
+const high = orders.filter((order) => order.bucket === "high");
+const low = orders.filter((order) => order.bucket === "low");
+
+const summary = {
+  total: orders.length,
+  high: high.length,
+  low: low.length,
+  highTotalAmount: Math.round(high.reduce((sum, order) => sum + order.amount, 0) * 100) / 100,
+  lowTotalAmount: Math.round(low.reduce((sum, order) => sum + order.amount, 0) * 100) / 100,
+};
+
+console.log(
+  `Order buckets — total: ${summary.total}, high value: ${summary.high}, low value: ${summary.low}`
+);
+
+return [{ json: summary }];
